30	
31	Continuous-water exposure, lava burn, and the oxygen/drowning meter all live only
32	in transient `GameTimers`/state. Saving, reloading, or respawning resets them
33	(breath restores to full). The status-effect **accumulators** (`effectRegenTimer`,
34	`effectPoisonTimer`) are likewise transient — but the effects **themselves**
35	(`state.effects`) persist via the `effects` field above and survive a reload.
36	
37	### Version history
38	
39	- **v12** (dedicated armor storage) — fixes a bug where equipped armor stayed in the inventory and occupied a hotbar/storage slot. `equippedArmor` is no longer a by-id reference into `inventorySlots` but the worn pieces themselves, persisted per slot as `SavedSlot` (via `serializeEquippedArmor`/`restoreEquippedArmor`, reusing the shared `restoreSlot` validation). `migrateSaveV11toV12` moves each legacy by-id equip out of `inventorySlots` into the armor record (preserving durability/enchantments/customName; equips whose item isn't found or isn't valid armor are dropped; a legacy `inventoryCounts`-only save just drops the equip map and keeps the items). No worldgen change — **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** and `SAVE_KEY` is unchanged.
40	- **v11** (anvil rename & Mending) — adds an optional `customName` on each `SavedSlot` (the anvil rename). `migrateSaveV10toV11` is a pure version bump (absent ⇒ no custom name). `customName` rides the existing `inventorySlotsSnapshot`/`restoreSlot` path (shared by inventory and chests), is restored on durable gear only, and is trimmed + capped to `CUSTOM_NAME_MAX_LEN` with blanks dropped. The **Mending** enchantment adds no new field — it rides the existing per-slot `enchantments` (and its `mending` id is added to the save validator's known-id set). The **Anvil** and **Grindstone** items save by string id and their `BlockId`s are **appended** so saved block ids never shift. No worldgen change — both are craft-only — so **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8**.
41	- **v10** (Hardcore) — adds optional top-level `hardcore` and `gameOver` booleans. `migrateSaveV9toV10` is a pure version bump (both absent ⇒ a normal, non-hardcore world, via `restoreHardcore`/`restoreGameOver`). `hardcore` is immutable (forces Survival + Hard, locks both switchers); `gameOver` is the persisted permadeath flag, so a dead hardcore world reloads into its spectator state rather than respawning (the transient `isDead`/`respawnTimer` remain session-only). No worldgen change — so **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8**.
```

1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Fixed
8	
9	- **Equipped armor no longer occupies a hotbar/inventory slot.** Armor was equipped _by reference_ — the worn piece stayed in the inventory and kept occupying its slot (and rendered twice: once in the hotbar/grid, once in the armor column). Armor now has **dedicated storage**: equipping **moves** the piece out of the inventory into its armor slot (freeing the slot, ending the double-render), and unequipping returns it to a free inventory slot (refused when the inventory is full, so nothing is lost). `EquippedArmor` now holds the worn `InventorySlot` instances; the defense/durability/Mending seams read them directly (no more find-by-id, which also removes the duplicate-piece ambiguity). The save schema bumps **v11 → v12** — `migrateSaveV11toV12` moves each legacy by-id equip out of `inventorySlots` into the armor record (preserving durability/enchantments/customName) — with **no worldgen change** (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8)
10	
11	### Added
12	
13	- **Equipped armor now shows on the player** in third-person (press **V**). Each worn piece renders as a procedurally-built voxel "shell" over the matching body part (helmet → head, face mask → visor, neck protection → collar, chestplate → torso + shoulder caps, leggings → upper legs, boots → feet), parented to the limb pivots so it animates with the walk/attack gait, and tinted toward the ore it's crafted from. Renderer-only (`playerModel.ts` builds the shells, `playerVisuals.ts` toggles each by what's worn) — zero new assets, no engine/save change
14	- **Grindstone**: a new crafted station (**2 cobble + 2 planks**) — the anvil's inverse, on **zero new assets** (a procedurally-painted stone-wheel block, reusing existing synth) and **no save-format or worldgen change**. Right-click to open the grindstone panel; with **enchanted** gear selected in the hotbar, **Remove enchantments** strips them all and refunds **`GRINDSTONE_REFUND_XP_PER_LEVEL` (5) XP per level** removed. Pure logic in `lib/game/grindstone.ts`, a new `grindstoneStrip` command, and a `GrindstoneColumn` panel; the `Grindstone` `BlockId` is **appended** (id 54), craft-only, so no worldgen baseline change
15	- **Anvil**: a new crafted station (**3 gold ore + 4 cobble**) for maintaining and consolidating gear, on **zero new assets** (a procedurally-painted dark-iron block, reusing existing synth for its clang). The save schema bumps **v10 → v11** (additive `customName`), with **no worldgen change** (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8)
16	  - Right-click to open the anvil panel, which — like the enchanting table — acts on the **selected hotbar** tool/weapon/armor and spends XP levels. Three actions: **Combine** a duplicate (adds the two durability bars + a bonus and **merges enchantments** at the higher level, consuming the duplicate); **Repair** with the item's tier material (e.g. diamond ore for a diamond sword, cobble for a stone pickaxe) for a chunk of durability without a second copy; and **Rename** (the custom name shows everywhere the item's label does)
17	  - **Architecture**: the new `Anvil` `BlockId` is **appended** (id 53) so saved block ids never shift, and it's craft-only so no worldgen baseline changes. Pure logic lives in `lib/game/anvil.ts` (`combineSlots`, `mergeEnchantments`, `findSacrificeIndex`, `materialRepair`, `sanitizeCustomName`), driven by new `anvilCombine`/`anvilRepair`/`anvilRename` commands. The anvil station id is threaded through the **engine/UI** `craftingStation` union only (not `Recipe.station`); the panel is a new `AnvilColumn`. A per-item `customName` (additive v11 field, durable gear only, trimmed + capped) persists the rename, surfaced everywhere via a shared `displayName` helper
18	- **Mending enchantment**: a fifth enchantment for the existing enchanting table, on **zero new assets** and **no worldgen change**. Applied to any durable tool/weapon/armor (like Unbreaking), it's **binary** (max level 1) and diverts the XP you pick up into **repairing** the gear you're holding or wearing instead of banking it — `MENDING_REPAIR_PER_XP` (2) durability per XP point, the held item first, then equipped armor. Implemented as one seam (`mendXp` in `enchantments.ts`) read once in `awardXp`, so XP earning, the HUD, and the sound still react to the gross pickup. The `mending` id rides the existing per-slot `enchantments`, so **no save-format bump** is needed for it
19	- **Hardcore mode**: a per-world **permadeath** toggle on the new-world form, all on **zero new assets**. The save schema bumps **v9 → v10** (additive `hardcore` + `gameOver`), with **no worldgen change**
20	  - **Locked to Survival + Hard.** A hardcore world forces the game mode to Survival and the difficulty to Hard and **disables both pause-menu switchers** (immutable for the world's life), so there's no flipping to Creative or lowering the difficulty to cheat death. Its hearts render in a darker **withered** variant (a recolour of the procedural heart sprite)
21	  - **Death is permanent → spectate the dead world.** On death there is no respawn: the player flips to a **Spectator** free camera over their dead world (mobs keep roaming but can't touch them) and a **Game Over** screen offers _Spectate World_ (minimizes so you can fly around), _Back to Worlds_, or _Delete World_. The `gameOver` flag persists, so reloading a dead world resumes spectating — it can never be played again
22	  - **Architecture**: `hardcore` is a per-world boolean (`WorldMeta` + a v10 `SaveData` field + `migrateSaveV9toV10`), resolved first in the engine so it overrides mode/difficulty. The two lethal damage seams divert to a new `GameEngine.triggerGameOver()` (sets `gameOver`, switches to spectator, leaves `isDead` cleared so the respawn screen never shows); `switchGameMode`/`switchDifficulty` no-op under hardcore (the death transition sets the mode directly); the shell force-saves on the `gameOver` event. New `GameOverScreen` component + a `GameShell` delete-world path
23	- **Difficulty**: the game gains Minecraft's difficulty axis — **Peaceful**, **Easy**, **Normal**, and **Hard** — orthogonal to the game mode, picked at world creation and switchable any time from the pause menu, all on **zero new assets**. The save schema bumps **v8 → v9** (additive), with **no worldgen change**
24	  - **Peaceful**: no hostile mobs spawn (and switching to Peaceful instantly despawns every hostile already around — a safe panic button, the boss included), health regenerates **twice as fast**, and an empty hunger bar never hurts you — full survival minus the danger, distinct from Creative
25	  - **Easy / Normal / Hard** scale two things together: **mob hit damage** (×0.5 / ×1 / ×1.5, applied at the strike — the per-mob templates are never mutated) and **hostile spawning** (cadence + the concurrent cap, ×16 → 8 / 16 / 24)
26	  - **Starvation** (new): an empty hunger bar now has a consequence — half a heart every few seconds, down to a difficulty-scaled floor. Easy stops at 5 hearts, Normal at half a heart, and **Hard can starve you to death**; Peaceful never starves. (This changes existing **Normal** worlds: previously a fully-empty hunger bar did nothing.)
27	  - **Architecture**: a new `lib/game/difficulties.ts` holds the `Difficulty` union plus **accessor functions** (`hostilesSpawn`, `mobDamageMultiplier`, `hostileSpawnIntervalScale`, `hostileCapScale`, `regenIntervalScale`, `starves`/`starvationFloorHp`) that each system reads, mirroring the game-mode predicate pattern. Difficulty rides the same per-world plumbing as `gameMode` (`WorldMeta` + a v9 `SaveData` field + `migrateSaveV8toV9`); the spawn gate keys **only** on difficulty and the damage/threat gate **only** on game mode, so the two axes stay independent (Peaceful stops spawns even in Creative; Creative is invulnerable even on Hard). New `setDifficulty` command (`GameEngine.switchDifficulty`); new `STARVATION_INTERVAL_SECONDS`/`STARVATION_HP` tunables
28	- **Game modes**: the game gains Minecraft's four ways to play — **Survival** (the original), **Creative**, **Adventure**, and **Spectator** — as a single per-player axis you pick at world creation and can switch any time from the pause menu, all on **zero new assets**. The save schema bumps **v7 → v8** (additive), with **no worldgen change**
29	  - **Creative**: fly (double-tap **Space**, then Space/crouch to rise/descend), take no damage and never hunger, break any block instantly, and place blocks without consuming them. The recipe book is replaced by a searchable **palette of every item** — click one to pull a stack into your inventory. The survival HUD bars hide
30	  - **Adventure**: survival in every respect (damage, hunger, mobs, combat, doors/chests/trading) **except you can't break or place blocks**, so a built world stays intact
31	  - **Spectator**: a free camera — fly through terrain (noclip), invisible and invulnerable, with no interaction at all; hostiles ignore you entirely and the HUD/hotbar hide
32	  - **Switching** modes mid-game (pause menu) refills your bars and clears any pending hazard damage, sets flight to match the mode, and lifts you out of a wall when leaving Spectator — so it's always safe to flip between them
33	  - **Architecture**: a new `lib/game/gameModes.ts` holds the `GameMode` union plus **intent predicates** (`takesDamage`, `canEditBlocks`, `freeBuild`, `canInteract`, `canFly`, `isNoclip`, `usesInventory`, `mobsThreaten`) that each system gates on, rather than comparing the raw id — so the four modes share one set of seams (the damage chokepoints in `playerLife.ts`, the block edits in `mining.ts`, aggro in `mobAI.ts`, flight/noclip in `playerMotion.ts`). `gameMode` rides the existing per-world `worldType` plumbing (`WorldMeta` + a v8 `SaveData` field + `migrateSaveV7toV8`); unlike `worldType` it's the _current_, switchable value, and the transient `isFlying` is never serialized. New `setGameMode`/`toggleFlight`/`creativeGiveItem` commands; new `FLY_SPEED`/`FLY_DOUBLE_TAP_WINDOW_SECONDS` tunables
34	
35	### Changed
36	
37	- **Pause menu reorganized into tabs**: the Esc menu had grown into one long scrolling column (action buttons, the game-mode grid, volume sliders, the skin picker, and a 12-row controls list). It's now split into **Game** (save / load / reset / quit + game mode), **Options** (sound + appearance), and **Controls** (the key reference), with **Back to Game** pinned on top. tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Kk6hVVMhw9H7hQMWuECE5i
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/combat.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Melee attack at the mob nearest the crosshair within reach. Returns the kind\n * of the mob hit (or null); the caller (engine) handles death drops and durability.\n */\nexport function tryAttackMob(state: GameState, damage: number, onMobKilled: (index: number) => void, reach = ATTACK_REACH): MobKind | null {\n  const { position } = state.player;\n  const bestIndex = findAimedMobIndex(state, reach);\n  if (bestIndex < 0) return null;\n  const mob = state.mobs[bestIndex];\n  mob.hp -= damage;\n\n  scratchKnock.copy(mob.position).sub(position).setY(0);\n  if (scratchKnock.lengthSq() > 0.0001) {\n    scratchKnock.normalize();\n    mob.direction.copy(scratchKnock);\n    mob.position.addScaledVector(scratchKnock, 0.75);\n    mob.position.y += 0.12;\n  }",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Melee attack at the mob nearest the crosshair within reach. Returns the kind\n * of the mob hit (or null); the caller (engine) handles death drops and durability.\n * `knockback` is the extra horizontal impulse from the Knockback enchantment (0 by default).\n */\nexport function tryAttackMob(state: GameState, damage: number, onMobKilled: (index: number) => void, reach = ATTACK_REACH, knockback = 0): MobKind | null {\n  const { position } = state.player;\n  const bestIndex = findAimedMobIndex(state, reach);\n  if (bestIndex < 0) return null;\n  const mob = state.mobs[bestIndex];\n  mob.hp -= damage;\n\n  scratchKnock.copy(mob.position).sub(position).setY(0);\n  if (scratchKnock.lengthSq() > 0.0001) {\n    scratchKnock.normalize();\n    mob.direction.copy(scratchKnock);\n    mob.position.addScaledVector(scratchKnock, MELEE_KNOCKBACK_IMPULSE + knockback);\n    mob.position.y += 0.12;\n  }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobLoot.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Rolls the drop table for a mob kind. Returns one entry per item that yielded a\n * positive count; the engine adds each to the inventory. `rng` is injectable so\n * tests get deterministic counts (0 → min, ~1 → max).\n */\nexport function rollMobDrops(kind: MobKind, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {\n  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];\n  for (const entry of MOB_DROPS[kind]) {\n    if (entry.chance !== undefined && clampUnit(rng()) >= entry.chance) continue;\n    const span = entry.max - entry.min + 1;\n    const count = entry.min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * span);\n    if (count > 0) drops.push({ itemId: entry.itemId, count });\n  }\n  return drops;\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Rolls the drop table for a mob kind. Returns one entry per item that yielded a\n * positive count; the engine adds each to the inventory. `rng` is injectable so\n * tests get deterministic counts (0 → min, ~1 → max). `lootingLevel` (from the\n * Looting enchantment on the killing weapon) adds a random bonus count — up to\n * `level × LOOTING_BONUS_PER_LEVEL` extra — to each entry that already dropped.\n */\nexport function rollMobDrops(kind: MobKind, rng: () => number, lootingLevel = 0): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {\n  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];\n  for (const entry of MOB_DROPS[kind]) {\n    if (entry.chance !== undefined && clampUnit(rng()) >= entry.chance) continue;\n    const span = entry.max - entry.min + 1;\n    let count = entry.min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * span);\n    if (count > 0 && lootingLevel > 0) {\n      count += Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * (lootingLevel * LOOTING_BONUS_PER_LEVEL + 1));\n    }\n    if (count > 0) drops.push({ itemId: entry.itemId, count });\n  }\n  return drops;\n}"
}

224	## Progression — mining & combat reach
225	
226	`MINE_REACH`, `MINING_RATE`, `BARE_HAND_MINE_POWER`, `FIST_DAMAGE`, `ATTACK_REACH`,
227	`ATTACK_AIM_DOT`, `SPEAR_MELEE_REACH`, `SPEAR_THROW_SPEED`,
228	`SPEAR_THROW_GRAVITY`, `SPEAR_THROW_LIFETIME_SECONDS`,
229	`SPEAR_STUCK_SECONDS`, `SPEAR_THROW_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`, `SPEAR_HIT_RADIUS`.
230	
231	Read by `systems/mining.ts`, `systems/combat.ts`, and `systems/spears.ts`.
232	`MINING_RATE` × a tool's
233	`minePower` (from `items.ts`) ÷ block hardness = break time, so this constant scales
234	_all_ mining globally while item tiers scale it per-tool. Note the deliberate
235	asymmetry: `MINE_REACH` (7) is longer than `ATTACK_REACH` (4.5) — you can dig
236	farther than you can punch. `ATTACK_AIM_DOT` (0.89) is how precisely the crosshair
237	must point at a mob to hit it — lower is more forgiving. The per-ore **tool-tier
238	gate** itself lives in `systems/mining.ts` (`canMineBlock`), not config. Spears
239	override only melee reach; their projectile speed, gravity, lifetime, cooldown,
240	terrain embed duration, and collision radius are global, while tier
241	damage/durability live in `items.ts`.

{
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(enchant): add Knockback and Looting for weapons\n\nMelee combat had no enchantment that changed how a hit lands or what a\nkill yields. Add two weapon enchantments: Knockback shoves the target\nfurther on a melee hit, and Looting rolls a bonus count on each mob drop\nfrom the killing weapon.\n\nKnockback reads off the held weapon at the tryAttackMob seam, scaling the\nshove; the base impulse is promoted from a magic 0.75 to the named\nMELEE_KNOCKBACK_IMPULSE tunable so the enchant adds to a documented base.\nLooting threads an optional level into rollMobDrops (default 0 keeps every\nexisting caller and test unchanged) and adds a randomized bonus to each\nentry that already dropped, read off the held weapon at removeMobAt.\n\nBoth ids ride the existing per-slot enchantments field (added to the\ncompile-enforced VALID_ENCHANT_IDS), so no save bump and WORLDGEN_VERSION\nstays 8.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1",
  "description": "Commit 2"
}
```

}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Items a broken block yields. The default is its single `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` entry;\n * grass occasionally also drops a seed (the natural seed source), mature wheat\n * drops wheat plus 1–2 seeds, and leaves occasionally drop a sapling (their only\n * yield — the renewable tree source). `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests.\n */\nexport function rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {\n  if (isDoorBlock(block)) return [{ itemId: \"door\", count: 1 }];\n  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];\n  const base = BLOCK_TO_SLOT[block];\n  if (base) drops.push({ itemId: base, count: 1 });",
  "new_string": "/** Ores whose mined yield the Fortune enchantment multiplies (their `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` item is the drop). */\nconst FORTUNE_ORE_BLOCKS = new Set<BlockId>([\n  BlockId.CoalOre,\n  BlockId.SliverOre,\n  BlockId.RubyOre,\n  BlockId.GoldOre,\n  BlockId.SapphireOre,\n  BlockId.DiamondOre\n]);\n\n/**\n * Items a broken block yields. The default is its single `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` entry;\n * grass occasionally also drops a seed (the natural seed source), mature wheat\n * drops wheat plus 1–2 seeds, and leaves occasionally drop a sapling (their only\n * yield — the renewable tree source). `fortuneLevel` (from the Fortune tool used)\n * rolls a bonus on **ore** drops only. `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests.\n */\nexport function rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng: () => number, fortuneLevel = 0): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {\n  if (isDoorBlock(block)) return [{ itemId: \"door\", count: 1 }];\n  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];\n  const base = BLOCK_TO_SLOT[block];\n  if (base) {\n    let count = 1;\n    if (fortuneLevel > 0 && FORTUNE_ORE_BLOCKS.has(block)) {\n      count += Math.floor(rng() * (fortuneLevel * FORTUNE_BONUS_PER_LEVEL + 1));\n    }\n    drops.push({ itemId: base, count });\n  }"
}
```

}

72	function run(engine: GameEngine, seconds: number, frame: FrameInput = input()): void {
73	  const dt = 1 / 60;
74	  for (let t = 0; t < seconds; t += dt) engine.step(dt, frame);
75	}
76	
77	/**
78	 * The world boots at dawn (daylight 0.05), so the initial hostiles aggro
79	 * immediately. Tests about other mechanics clear them and move to midday.
80	 */
81	function calmDaytime(engine: GameEngine): void {
82	  engine.state.mobs = engine.state.mobs.filter((mob) => !mob.hostile);
83	  engine.state.dayClock = 60;

  "new_string": "    state.player.position.y = groundY + 3.95;\n    state.player.velocity.set(0, 0, 0);\n    state.player.onGround = false;\n    run(engine, 1);\n    expect(engine.state.hearts).toBeLessThan(MAX_HEARTS);\n  });\n\n  test(\"Feather Falling boots soften fall damage\", () => {\n    // Same fall, same armor defense — only the enchantment differs — so any gap\n    // is Feather Falling's doing, not the boots' base armor.\n    const fallDamage = (boots: ReturnType<typeof createSlot>): number => {\n      const engine = makeEngine();\n      calmDaytime(engine);\n      run(engine, 1); // settle on the ground\n      const { state } = engine;\n      state.equippedArmor = { ...state.equippedArmor, boots };\n      state.hearts = MAX_HEARTS;\n      state.player.position.y += 16; // a hard fall, deep in the damage window\n      state.player.velocity.set(0, 0, 0);\n      state.player.onGround = false;\n      run(engine, 1.5); // long enough to land (no regen interval elapses)\n      return MAX_HEARTS - state.hearts;\n    };\n    const plain = fallDamage(createSlot(\"boots\", 1));\n    let ffBoots = createSlot(\"boots\", 1);\n    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i += 1) ffBoots = applyEnchant(ffBoots, \"feather_falling\"); // level 3\n    const softened = fallDamage(ffBoots);\n    expect(plain).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n    expect(softened).toBeLessThan(plain);\n  });"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { BlockId } from \"@/lib/world\";\nimport { FORTUNE_BONUS_PER_LEVEL } from \"@/lib/game/config\";\nimport { rollBlockDrops } from \"@/lib/game/items\";\n\n/** Count of `itemId` across a drop list (0 if absent). */\nfunction dropCount(drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }>, itemId: string): number {\n  return drops.find((d) => d.itemId === itemId)?.count ?? 0;\n}\n\ndescribe(\"rollBlockDrops Fortune\", () => {\n  test(\"Fortune adds a bonus to ore drops, scaled by level\", () => {\n    const rng = () => 0.999; // max bonus roll\n    // No Fortune → a single diamond.\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.DiamondOre, rng), \"diamond_ore\")).toBe(1);\n    // Fortune 3 → 1 + floor(0.999 × (3 × 1 + 1)) = 1 + 3.\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.DiamondOre, rng, 3), \"diamond_ore\")).toBe(1 + 3 * FORTUNE_BONUS_PER_LEVEL);\n    // Coal ore (drops the coal item) is an ore too.\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.CoalOre, rng, 3), \"coal\")).toBe(1 + 3 * FORTUNE_BONUS_PER_LEVEL);\n  });\n\n  test(\"Fortune does not multiply non-ore blocks\", () => {\n    const rng = () => 0.999;\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.Stone, rng, 3), \"stone\")).toBe(1);\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.Planks, rng, 3), \"planks\")).toBe(1);\n    // Leaves still drop only their chance sapling — Fortune leaves it untouched.\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.Leaves, rng, 3), \"sapling\")).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  test(\"Fortune level 0 (default) is the plain single ore drop\", () => {\n    const rng = () => 0.999;\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.GoldOre, rng, 0), \"gold_ore\")).toBe(1);\n    expect(dropCount(rollBlockDrops(BlockId.GoldOre, rng), \"gold_ore\")).toBe(1);\n  });\n});\n"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/save-format.md",
  "old_string": "- **Unreleased (no save bump)** — new enchantments (**Power**, **Punch**, **Knockback**, **Looting**) ride the existing per-slot `enchantments` field; their ids are added to the save validator's known-id set (`VALID_ENCHANT_IDS`, a compile-enforced `Record<EnchantmentId, true>`). No new field, no new `BlockId`, no worldgen change — the save schema stays **v12** and **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** (the same additive-id pattern as Mending). Older builds silently drop unknown ids on load.",
  "new_string": "- **Unreleased (no save bump)** — new enchantments (**Power**, **Punch**, **Knockback**, **Looting**, **Fortune**, **Feather Falling**) ride the existing per-slot `enchantments` field; their ids are added to the save validator's known-id set (`VALID_ENCHANT_IDS`, a compile-enforced `Record<EnchantmentId, true>`). No new field, no new `BlockId`, no worldgen change — the save schema stays **v12** and **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** (the same additive-id pattern as Mending). Older builds silently drop unknown ids on load."
}

377	    const engine = makeEngine();
378	    calmDaytime(engine);
379	    run(engine, 1); // settle
380	    const { state } = engine;
381	    const px = Math.floor(state.player.position.x);
382	    const py = Math.floor(state.player.position.y) - 1;
383	    const pz = Math.floor(state.player.position.z);
384	    // Pin the block underfoot to a guaranteed-drop block: the spawn surface can
385	    // be leaves (a tree canopy), which now drop only an occasional sapling, so a
386	    // generic "yields a drop" assertion would be flaky.
387	    state.blockChanges.set(px, py, pz, BlockId.Dirt);
388	    expect(state.world.get(px, py, pz)).not.toBe(BlockId.Air);
389	
390	    // Center the player in the cell: a ray origin exactly on a cell boundary
391	    // is ambiguous in the DDA and may target the diagonal neighbor.
392	    state.player.position.x = px + 0.5;
393	    state.player.position.z = pz + 0.5;
394	    state.player.pitch = -Math.PI / 2 + 0.02; // look straight down
395	    const before = countsById(state.inventory);
396	    run(engine, 4, input({ leftMouseHeld: true, pointerLocked: true }));
397	
398	    expect(state.world.get(px, py, pz)).toBe(BlockId.Air);
399	    const after = countsById(state.inventory);
400	    // The broken block (grass/dirt/sand/...) maps to a drop that increments.
401	    const gained = [...after.entries()].some(([id, count]) => count > (before.get(id) ?? 0));
402	    expect(gained).toBe(true);
403	    expect(state.blockChanges.changes().length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
404	  });

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/playerLife.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Applies armor-mitigated damage (always at least 1) and wears equipped armor.\n * Returns true when the hit was lethal — the engine emits the death event.\n * Creative/Spectator are invulnerable, so every damage source no-ops here.\n */\nexport function applyDamageWithArmor(state: GameState, amount: number, rng?: () => number): boolean {\n  if (state.isDead || !takesDamage(state.gameMode)) return false;\n  const value = Math.max(0, Math.floor(amount));\n  if (value <= 0) return false;\n\n  state.equippedArmor = consumeEquippedArmorDurability(state.equippedArmor, 1, rng) ?? state.equippedArmor;\n  const reduction = armorReduction(state.equippedArmor);\n  const mitigated = Math.max(1, Math.floor(value * (1 - reduction)));",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Applies armor-mitigated damage (always at least 1) and wears equipped armor.\n * The Resistance effect further scales the post-armor amount (combat damage only —\n * environmental/poison damage uses the unmitigated/non-lethal paths below).\n * Returns true when the hit was lethal — the engine emits the death event.\n * Creative/Spectator are invulnerable, so every damage source no-ops here.\n */\nexport function applyDamageWithArmor(state: GameState, amount: number, rng?: () => number): boolean {\n  if (state.isDead || !takesDamage(state.gameMode)) return false;\n  const value = Math.max(0, Math.floor(amount));\n  if (value <= 0) return false;\n\n  state.equippedArmor = consumeEquippedArmorDurability(state.equippedArmor, 1, rng) ?? state.equippedArmor;\n  const reduction = armorReduction(state.equippedArmor);\n  const mitigated = Math.max(1, Math.floor(value * (1 - reduction) * resistanceMultiplier(state)));"
}
```

120	/** Left half of `full` over `container` — the classic half-heart/half-drumstick. */
121	function halfIcon(full: Uint8ClampedArray, container: Uint8ClampedArray): Uint8ClampedArray {
122	  const out = new Uint8ClampedArray(container);
123	  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
124	    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE / 2; x += 1) {
125	      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
126	      if (full[i + 3] === 0) continue;
127	      out[i] = full[i];
128	      out[i + 1] = full[i + 1];
129	      out[i + 2] = full[i + 2];
130	      out[i + 3] = full[i + 3];
131	    }
132	  }
133	  return out;
134	}

  "new_string": "describe(\"modifiers\", () => {\n  test(\"speedMultiplier and strengthBonus reflect active effects\", () => {\n    const state = makeState();\n    expect(speedMultiplier(state)).toBe(1);\n    expect(strengthBonus(state)).toBe(0);\n    addEffect(state, \"speed\", 10);\n    addEffect(state, \"strength\", 10);\n    expect(speedMultiplier(state)).toBe(EFFECT_SPEED_MULTIPLIER);\n    expect(strengthBonus(state)).toBe(EFFECT_STRENGTH_BONUS);\n  });\n\n  test(\"haste, resistance, and jump-boost modifiers reflect active effects\", () => {\n    const state = makeState();\n    expect(hasteMultiplier(state)).toBe(1);\n    expect(resistanceMultiplier(state)).toBe(1);\n    expect(jumpBoostBonus(state)).toBe(0);\n    addEffect(state, \"haste\", 10);\n    addEffect(state, \"resistance\", 10);\n    addEffect(state, \"jump_boost\", 10);\n    expect(hasteMultiplier(state)).toBe(EFFECT_HASTE_MULTIPLIER);\n    expect(resistanceMultiplier(state)).toBe(EFFECT_RESISTANCE_MULTIPLIER);\n    expect(jumpBoostBonus(state)).toBe(EFFECT_JUMP_BOOST_VELOCITY);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"Resistance scope\", () => {\n  test(\"reduces armor-mitigated combat damage but leaves unmitigated hazards alone\", () => {\n    const plain = makeState({ hearts: 20, equippedArmor: createEmptyArmorEquipment() });\n    const resisted = makeState({ hearts: 20, equippedArmor: createEmptyArmorEquipment() });\n    addEffect(resisted, \"resistance\", 60);\n    applyDamageWithArmor(plain, 10);\n    applyDamageWithArmor(resisted, 10);\n    expect(20 - resisted.hearts).toBeLessThan(20 - plain.hearts); // Resistance softened the combat hit\n\n    // Lava/void/starvation flow through applyUnmitigatedDamage, which Resistance must not touch.\n    const hazardPlain = makeState({ hearts: 20 });\n    const hazardResisted = makeState({ hearts: 20 });\n    addEffect(hazardResisted, \"resistance\", 60);\n    applyUnmitigatedDamage(hazardPlain, 6);\n    applyUnmitigatedDamage(hazardResisted, 6);\n    expect(hazardResisted.hearts).toBe(hazardPlain.hearts);\n  });\n});"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts",
  "old_string": "  test(\"mining is gated on pointer lock and the inventory being closed\", () => {\n    const engine = makeEngine();\n    calmDaytime(engine);\n    run(engine, 1);",
  "new_string": "  test(\"the Haste effect speeds up mining progress\", () => {\n    // Same block + tool + duration; only the effect differs, and the run is short\n    // enough that the (hard) stone never breaks, so progress accumulates.\n    const progressAfter = (haste: boolean): number => {\n      const engine = makeEngine();\n      calmDaytime(engine);\n      run(engine, 1); // settle\n      const { state } = engine;\n      const px = Math.floor(state.player.position.x);\n      const py = Math.floor(state.player.position.y) - 1;\n      const pz = Math.floor(state.player.position.z);\n      state.blockChanges.set(px, py, pz, BlockId.Stone);\n      state.player.position.x = px + 0.5;\n      state.player.position.z = pz + 0.5;\n      state.player.pitch = -Math.PI / 2 + 0.02; // look straight down\n      state.inventory[state.selectedSlot] = createSlot(\"wood_pickaxe\", 1);\n      if (haste) addEffect(state, \"haste\", 60);\n      run(engine, 0.5, input({ leftMouseHeld: true, pointerLocked: true }));\n      return state.mining.progress;\n    };\n    const plain = progressAfter(false);\n    const hasted = progressAfter(true);\n    expect(plain).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n    expect(hasted).toBeGreaterThan(plain);\n  });\n\n  test(\"mining is gated on pointer lock and the inventory being closed\", () => {\n    const engine = makeEngine();\n    calmDaytime(engine);\n    run(engine, 1);"
}

113	## Status effects & potions
114	
115	`EFFECT_SPEED_DURATION`/`EFFECT_SPEED_MULTIPLIER`,
116	`EFFECT_STRENGTH_DURATION`/`EFFECT_STRENGTH_BONUS`,
117	`EFFECT_REGEN_DURATION`/`EFFECT_REGEN_INTERVAL`/`EFFECT_REGEN_HP`,
118	`EFFECT_FIRE_RESIST_DURATION`, `EFFECT_WATER_BREATHING_DURATION`,
119	`POISON_DURATION`/`POISON_INTERVAL`/`POISON_HP`/`POISON_FLOOR_HP`,
120	`ROTTEN_FLESH_POISON_CHANCE`.
121	
122	Read by `systems/statusEffects.ts` (and the seams it feeds: `playerMotion.ts`
123	for speed, the melee dispatch in `GameEngine.ts` for strength, and the gated
124	`tickLavaExposure`/`tickOxygen` for fire-resist/water-breathing). The
125	`*_DURATION` values are how long a drunk potion lasts (default Minecraft-ish:
126	buffs 3:00, Regeneration 0:45). **Strength** adds `EFFECT_STRENGTH_BONUS` (3) flat
127	melee damage; **Speed** multiplies movement by `EFFECT_SPEED_MULTIPLIER` (1.2);
128	**Regeneration** heals `EFFECT_REGEN_HP` (1) every `EFFECT_REGEN_INTERVAL` (1.5 s)
129	on its **own** accumulator, ignoring the hunger gate. **Poison** ticks `POISON_HP`
130	(1) every `POISON_INTERVAL` (1.25 s) but floors at `POISON_FLOOR_HP` (1) so it can
131	never kill; eating rotten flesh inflicts it with probability
132	`ROTTEN_FLESH_POISON_CHANCE` (0.8) for `POISON_DURATION` (8 s). Brewing reagent
133	costs are balanced in `recipes.ts`, not here. Lengthen the buffs or cut the poison
134	odds to make the system gentler; the reagent map is the economic dial.
```

315:## Brewing and potions
317:Once you've gathered some gold, craft a **brewing stand** (3 cobble + 1 gold ore),
318:place it, and right-click it to open the panel in **brewing** mode — just like a
319:furnace, the potion recipes unlock while the stand is open.
321:Every potion starts with a **glass bottle** (3 glass → 3 bottles, on the basic
322:grid). Add one reagent at the stand to brew a potion:
324:| Potion          | Reagent   | While active                          |

335	back up. Full numbers are in the [reference](reference.md#status-effects).

Older builds silently drop unknown ids on load.",
  "new_string": "- **Unreleased (no save bump)** — new enchantments (**Power**, **Punch**, **Knockback**, **Looting**, **Fortune**, **Feather Falling**) ride the existing per-slot `enchantments` field, and new status effects (**Haste**, **Resistance**, **Jump Boost**) ride the existing top-level `effects` field. Their ids are added to the save validators' known-id sets (`VALID_ENCHANT_IDS` / `VALID_EFFECT_IDS`, both compile-enforced `Record<…, true>`). No new field, no new `BlockId`, no worldgen change — the save schema stays **v12** and **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** (the same additive-id pattern as Mending). Older builds silently drop unknown ids on load."
}

```json
{
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(brewing): add Haste, Resistance, and Jump Boost potions\n\nThe potion system had five buffs and no mining, defensive, or mobility\noption. Add three brewable potions — Haste (faster mining), Resistance\n(less combat damage), and Leaping/Jump Boost (higher jumps) — each an\nengine-only effect read at one seam.\n\nHaste multiplies mining progress in tickMining; Resistance scales the\npost-armor combat damage in applyDamageWithArmor only, leaving the\nunmitigated lava/void/poison paths alone (asserted in tests); Jump Boost\nadds to the jump launch velocity in tickPlayerMotion, tuned to stay under\nthe fall-damage threshold so leaping never self-damages. Each is brewed\nfrom an existing reagent, gets a procedurally-painted HUD icon and bottle\nsprite (no new assets, no renderer change), and rides the existing\ntop-level effects field (added to the compile-enforced VALID_EFFECT_IDS),\nso no save bump and WORLDGEN_VERSION stays 8.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -4",
  "description": "Commit 4"
}
```

8:## What is covered
18:| Save format           | `lib/game/save.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Round-trips (incl. v4→v5, v5→v6, and v6→v7 migrations, `lootedChests`, the `effects` field, `xp`, and per-slot `enchantments` — round-trips + unknown-id/garbage-value dropping and level clamping), legacy-shape parsing and durable-stack splitting, corrupt-data rejection, `restorePlayerPosition` keeps float coords but rejects non-finite/missing ones                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

> AGENT

The "Deepen progression" feature is complete on branch `feat/deepen-progression` — **5 commits, 31 files, +744/−80**, with the full CI gate green.

## What shipped

**6 new enchantments** (all one-seam additions, gated correctly via the new optional `itemIds` allow-list where needed):
- **Power / Punch** — bow arrow damage / knockback (bow-only)
- **Knockback / Looting** — melee shove / bonus mob drops (any weapon)
- **Fortune** — extra ore on mining (tools, ore-blocks only)
- **Feather Falling** — softer landings (boots-only)

**3 new potions / status effects** (engine-only, HUD icons + bottle sprites, no renderer changes):
- **Haste** (×1.4 mining), **Resistance** (×0.8 combat damage only — lava/void/poison untouched), **Leaping** (higher jumps, tuned under the fall-damage threshold)

**Key invariants held:** zero new binary assets, **no save-format bump** (new ids ride the existing `enchantments`/`effects` fields, validated by the compile-enforced `VALID_ENCHANT_IDS`/`VALID_EFFECT_IDS` totality maps), and **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8**.

## Verification (all green)
- `lint` · `typecheck` · `format:check` · **853 unit/integration/component tests** · `build`
- **17 Playwright e2e** smoke tests
- New tests cover every seam: enchant readers + `itemIds` gating, Looting/Fortune rolls, Knockback impulse, Feather Falling/Haste/Jump Boost through the engine, Resistance's combat-only scope, and additive save round-trips.
- Docs updated with each change: `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `tuning.md`, `save-format.md`, `adding-content.md`, `testing.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Two notes
- **Not pushed / no PR opened** — per your repo policy (pushing & PRs are user-authorized) and your standing "review fixes: no push" preference. Say the word and I'll push and open the PR against `hutusi/main`.
- **One optional manual check:** the new effect HUD icons (gold star / steel shield / green up-arrow) and the three potion bottle tints are procedurally drawn — unit tests confirm they render as non-empty 16×16 sprites, but if you want them visually eyeballed, run `bun run dev`, brew one of each, and glance at the top-left effect readout.

Want me to push the branch and open the PR?

> AGENT

CI `verify` and `e2e` both passed. CodeRabbit (CHILL profile) raised 3 findings. My assessment:

1. **🟠 Major — Looting reads live `selectedSlot`, not the kill source** (`GameEngine.ts`) — **valid bug.** `removeMobAt` is the shared death handler for *all* kills (melee, thrown spears, arrows, explosions). For delayed kills, the player can swap items between the hit and the death, so the Looting bonus comes from the wrong weapon. Worth fixing.
2. **🟡 Minor — `jump_boost` label "Leaping" vs "Jump Boost"** (`ActiveEffects.tsx`) — **declining:** the repo's own convention is potion-flavored HUD labels (`speed → "Swiftness"`, not "Speed"). "Leaping" matches that precedent; changing only this one would make it the odd one out.
3. **🔵 Nitpick — test fixtures use `as unknown as` casts** — **declining:** that's the established pattern across the existing test suite (`statusEffects.test.ts`, the pre-existing `combat.test.ts` `makeState`); I only extended it. A typed builder is a heavy lift inconsistent with the rest.

Let me fix #1 properly — make Looting a **melee-only** enchant (matches Minecraft) by threading the level from the melee dispatch through the kill callback; every other death path gets 0.

{

  "old_string": "/**\n * Melee attack at the mob nearest the crosshair within reach. Returns the kind\n * of the mob hit (or null); the caller (engine) handles death drops and durability.\n * `knockback` is the extra horizontal impulse from the Knockback enchantment (0 by default).\n */\nexport function tryAttackMob(state: GameState, damage: number, onMobKilled: (index: number) => void, reach = ATTACK_REACH, knockback = 0): MobKind | null {\n  const { position } = state.player;\n  const bestIndex = findAimedMobIndex(state, reach);\n  if (bestIndex < 0) return null;\n  const mob = state.mobs[bestIndex];\n  mob.hp -= damage;\n\n  scratchKnock.copy(mob.position).sub(position).setY(0);\n  if (scratchKnock.lengthSq() > 0.0001) {\n    scratchKnock.normalize();\n    mob.direction.copy(scratchKnock);\n    mob.position.addScaledVector(scratchKnock, MELEE_KNOCKBACK_IMPULSE + knockback);\n    mob.position.y += 0.12;\n  }\n\n  if (mob.hp <= 0) onMobKilled(bestIndex);\n  return mob.kind;\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Melee attack at the mob nearest the crosshair within reach. Returns the kind\n * of the mob hit (or null); the caller (engine) handles death drops and durability.\n * `knockback` is the extra horizontal impulse from the Knockback enchantment (0 by\n * default); `lootingLevel` (the held weapon's Looting) is forwarded to the kill\n * callback so the drop roll uses the *killing* weapon, not whatever is held when a\n * delayed kill resolves — Looting is a melee enchant, so indirect kills pass 0.\n */\nexport function tryAttackMob(\n  state: GameState,\n  damage: number,\n  onMobKilled: (index: number, lootingLevel?: number) => void,\n  reach = ATTACK_REACH,\n  knockback = 0,\n  lootingLevel = 0\n): MobKind | null {\n  const { position } = state.player;\n  const bestIndex = findAimedMobIndex(state, reach);\n  if (bestIndex < 0) return null;\n  const mob = state.mobs[bestIndex];\n  mob.hp -= damage;\n\n  scratchKnock.copy(mob.position).sub(position).setY(0);\n  if (scratchKnock.lengthSq() > 0.0001) {\n    scratchKnock.normalize();\n    mob.direction.copy(scratchKnock);\n    mob.position.addScaledVector(scratchKnock, MELEE_KNOCKBACK_IMPULSE + knockback);\n    mob.position.y += 0.12;\n  }\n\n  if (mob.hp <= 0) onMobKilled(bestIndex, lootingLevel);\n  return mob.kind;\n}"
}

Looting on the held\n    // weapon rolls a bonus count per drop entry (0 for any non-Looting item).\n    const drops = mob.ageTimer <= 0 ? rollMobDrops(mob.kind, this.rng, lootingLevel(state.inventory[state.selectedSlot])) : [];",

I've reviewed CodeRabbit's feedback on PR #37 and acted on it. Here's the summary.

## CI status
Both required checks **passed**: `verify` (lint/typecheck/format/test/build) and `e2e`. CodeRabbit's status is also green. The PR is mergeable.

## The 3 findings

| # | Severity | Finding | My call |
|---|----------|---------|---------|
| 1 | 🟠 Major | Looting read live `selectedSlot` in `removeMobAt`, not the kill source | **Fixed** |
| 2 | 🟡 Minor | `jump_boost` HUD label "Leaping" vs "Jump Boost" | **Declined** |
| 3 | 🔵 Nitpick | Test fixtures use `as unknown as` casts | **Declined** |

**#1 was a real bug.** `removeMobAt` is the shared death handler for *all* kills — melee, thrown spears, arrows, explosions. Reading Looting off the currently-held slot there meant a delayed kill (throw spear → swap to a Looting sword → spear lands) applied the wrong bonus. I threaded the killing **melee** weapon's level through `tryAttackMob` into the kill callback; indirect kills now pass 0, matching Minecraft where Looting is melee-only. Added a test that the level is forwarded, and updated reference/tuning/CHANGELOG to say "melee kill."

**#2 declined** — the repo's own convention is potion-flavored HUD labels (`speed → "Swiftness"`, not "Speed"). "Leaping" matches that; switching only this one to "Jump Boost" would make it inconsistent. Easy to flip if you'd rather match the effect name — your call.

**#3 declined** — `as unknown as GameState` is the established stub pattern across the existing suite (`statusEffects.test.ts`, the pre-existing `combat.test.ts` `makeState`). A typed builder is a heavy lift inconsistent with the rest; I only extended the existing helper.

## State
- Fix committed **locally** as `4e716d2` (not pushed — per your "review fixes: check before push" preference).
- Full gate re-run green: **854 tests** (one new), lint/typecheck/format/build all pass.

=== CHANGELOG: top sections ===
5:## [Unreleased]

fatal: Needed a single revision

=== save-format current version ===
39:- **Unreleased (no save bump)** — new enchantments (**Power**, **Punch**, **Knockback**, **Looting**, **Fortune**, **Feather Falling**) ride the existing per-slot `enchantments` field, and new status effects (**Haste**, **Resistance**, **Jump Boost**) ride the existing top-level `effects` field. Their ids are added to the save validators' known-id sets (`VALID_ENCHANT_IDS` / `VALID_EFFECT_IDS`, both compile-enforced `Record<…, true>`). No new field, no new `BlockId`, no worldgen change — the save schema stays **v12** and **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** (the same additive-id pattern as Mending). Older builds silently drop unknown ids on load.
40:- **v12** (dedicated armor storage) — fixes a bug where equipped armor stayed in the inventory and occupied a hotbar/storage slot. `equippedArmor` is no longer a by-id reference into `inventorySlots` but the worn pieces themselves, persisted per slot as `SavedSlot` (via `serializeEquippedArmor`/`restoreEquippedArmor`, reusing the shared `restoreSlot` validation). `migrateSaveV11toV12` moves each legacy by-id equip out of `inventorySlots` into the armor record (preserving durability/enchantments/customName; equips whose item isn't found or isn't valid armor are dropped; a legacy `inventoryCounts`-only save just drops the equip map and keeps the items). No worldgen change — **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** and `SAVE_KEY` is unchanged.
54:- **v5** — adds optional `lootedChests` (opened/broken dungeon loot chests). `migrateSaveV4toV5` is a pure version bump (absent → no dungeon chests yet looted). Unlike v3/v4, **`SAVE_KEY` is bumped to `minecraft_save_v6`** here — not because of the save _shape_ (the field is additive) but because the dungeon worldgen changed the deterministic terrain baseline, so old block-diffs would index against the wrong world. Pre-dungeon saves are therefore discarded in practice; the migration keeps the version chain complete regardless. `readSave` chains v1 → v2 → v3 → v4 → v5 → v6 → v7 → v8 → v9 → v10 → v11 → v12.
```

Syncs the fork's `main` back to upstream — **58 commits** since the last sync (#9, living world & fishing). Everything is additive and backward-compatible; the save schema advances **v5 → v10** through a chain of pure version-bump migrations (`readSave` chains v1 → … → v10), so existing worlds load clean. CI is green on the fork's `main` (lint, typecheck, format, full unit suite, build, and Playwright e2e).

## What's included (oldest → newest)

- **Status effects, brewing & potions** (#28, save v5→v6) — six timed effects (Speed, Strength, Regeneration, Fire Resistance, Water Breathing, and a never-lethal Poison), a **Brewing Stand** station that reuses the recipe pipeline, and potions drunk with `F`. Each effect is read at exactly one existing seam.
- **XP & enchanting** (#29, save v6→v7) — banked XP from kills/ore/fishing (not lost on death), an **Enchanting Table** that spends XP levels on deterministic, player-chosen enchantments (Sharpness / Protection / Efficiency / Unbreaking, up to level 3). Enchantments are per-item-instance data riding the existing durability/snapshot plumbing.
- **Crafting recipe hints + 3 fixes** (#30) — unaffordable recipes show have/need per ingredient plus a "how to obtain it" hint (from a new `itemSources.ts`); plus fixes for player-position load validation, a block-atlas texture leak, and save-on-unmount.
- **Release v0.10.0** — rolls the above into a tagged release.
- **Game modes** (#31, save v7→v8) — Minecraft's four modes (**Survival / Creative / Adventure / Spectator**) as one per-player axis, picked at creation and switchable in the pause menu. Built on intent **predicates** (`takesDamage`/`canEditBlocks`/`canFly`/`isNoclip`/…) so all four share one set of seams. Creative gets an all-items palette + double-tap-Space flight.
- **Pause menu → tabs** (#32) — the Esc menu split into **Game / Options / Controls** (pure UI; no save/behavior change).
- **Difficulty** (#33, save v8→v9) — **Peaceful / Easy / Normal / Hard** as an axis orthogonal to game mode. Peaceful disables hostile spawns (and despawns existing ones) + faster regen; Easy/Normal/Hard scale mob damage and spawn cadence/cap; plus a new difficulty-scaled **hunger starvation** mechanic. Per-level numbers live as accessors in `difficulties.ts`.
- **Hardcore** (#34, save v9→v10) — a per-world **permadeath** toggle that locks Survival + Hard. On death the run ends: the player flips to a **Spectator** free-cam over their dead world with a **Game Over** screen (Spectate / Back to Worlds / Delete World); the dead state persists. Hearts render in a withered variant.

## Notes for review

- **Zero new binary assets** throughout — every sprite/texture is pixel-code, every sound is synthesized (the project's standing principle).
- **Save compatibility**: each step is an optional additive field with a pure migration; pre-feature saves restore with sensible defaults (e.g. `gameMode` → survival, `difficulty` → normal, `hardcore`/`gameOver` → false). No `WORLDGEN_VERSION` change in any of these.
- **Worth a manual gameplay pass** on the visual/interactive bits that headless tests can't fully cover: the Creative palette + flight, the brewing/enchanting panels, and especially Hardcore's death → spectator → Game Over flow.

Docs are updated alongside each feature (architecture, save-format, tuning, manual, reference, README, CHANGELOG).

=== CHANGELOG bottom (compare links?) ===
- Emergency unstuck (auto safeguard + `U` key)
- Shared 16×16 per-face block texture atlas with multi-layer terrain shading and ambient occlusion

### Changed

- World deepened to 150 blocks with wider cave generation
- Water rendered from both sides; players can stay submerged without unstuck teleport
- Game runtime refactored into modular systems (`lib/game/runtime/`) with split UI/styles
- Smooth terrain noise (fixes tall column artifacts); Plains-biome spawn priority
- Mob combat tightened: no hitting through blocks, vertical melee reach check, knockback

### Fixed

- Map persistence and player-stuck bugs (collision depenetration, load-time relocation safety)
- Startup freeze from world generation load
- Dark block undersides via brighter tiles and bounce lighting

## [0.1.0]

Initial Minecraft-like prototype: procedural voxel terrain, first-person movement with gravity and collisions, block breaking/placing, hotbar, and basic mobs. Built with Next.js, TypeScript, Three.js, and Bun.

1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Fixed
8	
9	- **Equipped armor no longer occupies a hotbar/inventory slot.** Armor was equipped _by reference_ — the worn piece stayed in the inventory and kept occupying its slot (and rendered twice: once in the hotbar/grid, once in the armor column). Armor now has **dedicated storage**: equipping **moves** the piece out of the inventory into its armor slot (freeing the slot, ending the double-render), and unequipping returns it to a free inventory slot (refused when the inventory is full, so nothing is lost). `EquippedArmor` now holds the worn `InventorySlot` instances; the defense/durability/Mending seams read them directly (no more find-by-id, which also removes the duplicate-piece ambiguity). The save schema bumps **v11 → v12** — `migrateSaveV11toV12` moves each legacy by-id equip out of `inventorySlots` into the armor record (preserving durability/enchantments/customName) — with **no worldgen change** (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8)
10

Fixed a **pre-existing** bug (user-reported): equipped armor occupied a hotbar slot because equipping was a by-reference marker (`equippedArmor[slot]=id`) leaving the item in the inventory (double-render + by-id ambiguity). Now `EquippedArmor = Record<ArmorSlot, InventorySlot|null>` (worn instances); `toggleEquipArmor` MOVES the piece out of inventory (swap-on-occupied), new `unequipArmor` moves it back (refused when full); `equippedDefense`/`armorReduction`/`consumeEquippedArmorDurability`/`mendXp` read the record directly; `syncEquippedArmor`/`unequipMissingArmor` deleted. Per-voxel light engine (`lib/world/lighting.ts`: packed sky+block 0–15, derived cache never serialized, full bake at load + Minecraft-style remove/refill `applyEdit` on every edit via the `blockChanges.set` chokepoint), mesher `aLight` attribute, and a `GameRenderer` `onBeforeCompile` shader patch that gates terrain by sky-exposure (caves dark) + adds block-light glow — **day/night via the existing scene lights, no uniform, no re-mesh** (refined the planned uDayFactor away). Torches (craft 4 from 1 wood, emit 14), deep-cave **lava** (worldgen-only, emit 15, burns on contact), **drowning** bubble meter (head-cell, coexists with the 60s body-immersion timer). **`SAVE_KEY` bumped v6→v7** (lava worldgen re-baseline; payload schema unchanged at v5 — nothing new persisted). Worldgen+meshing hashes re-rolled; structural probes stayed green because `placeLava` runs after `placeOres` (no RNG drift). All gates green (lint/typecheck/format/458 tests/build/e2e 10/10); **a human visual pass on the dark caves + torch/lava glow + drowning bubbles is still recommended** (per docs/testing.md, couldn't do it headless).
89	
90	The roadmap is now essentially exhausted — every major direction (UI, juice, chests, dungeons, endgame/ranged, upstream sync, caves & lighting) has shipped or is built. Likely next acts are non-feature: cut the long-pending release (package.json bump + roll `[Unreleased]` → a version + tag) once caves & lighting merges, or polish. The game has caves (now dark), ores, ~120 houses, 5 biomes, water+lava, 6 mobs + a boss, farming/breeding/cooking/beds, 7-tier gear + bow + spears, chests, dungeons, doors, torches, and per-voxel lighting. See [[review-fixes-no-push]] and [[no-pr-attribution]] for workflow preferences.
91

what's next do you recommend?

> AGENT

We just shipped v0.11.0 — and with it the game's combat/progression is now genuinely deep: 7 gear tiers, 11 enchantments, 9 potions/effects, an anvil/grindstone/enchanting loop, a boss. Three consecutive releases (#35 gear, #36 armor, #37 enchants/potions) have all been combat-and-gear focused.

So my read on the biggest gap: **the game is content-rich but goal-poor.** There's a huge amount to *do*, but almost nothing that gives players *direction* or rewards engaging with it — the only real objective is "beat the Dragon Lord." That's the highest-leverage thing to fix next, and it's a refreshing pivot away from more combat.

**My recommendation: Goals & progression meta — Advancements + Statistics.** A tree of advancements ("craft your first pickaxe," "enchant an item," "brew every potion," "survive a night," "defeat the Dragon Lord") plus a stats screen (blocks mined, mobs killed, distance walked, deaths). It makes *everything already in the game* pay off, guides new players through systems they'd otherwise miss, and is low-risk: additive save field, no worldgen change, mostly event-tracking (the engine already emits the events) + one new UI panel. It's the natural capstone after three content releases.

That said, the direction is yours — here are the credible alternatives:

I've kicked off two parallel探索 passes to ground the design — one mapping the engine's event/state/save/projection layer (the advancement triggers + stat counters + persistence), the other mapping the React shell (where the panel lives, the open/close flow, and the unlock-toast pattern). They're running in the background; I'll fold their findings into a concrete, scoped plan as soon as they report back.

While that runs, here's the shape I'm expecting to propose, so you can react early:

- **Statistics** — a `stats` counter map on `GameState` (persisted like `xp`, *not* cleared on death), incremented at the events the engine already emits (`blockBroken`, `mobDied`, `drankPotion`, `enchanted`, distance from the motion tick, `died`, etc.). Surfaced read-only to a stats screen.
- **Advancements** — a static registry of advancement definitions (id, title, description, icon, trigger condition, optional parent for a small tree), an `unlocked` id-set on `GameState` (also persisted, death-proof), checked at the same event chokepoint; a one-shot "just unlocked" projection drives a toast + sound.
- **UI** — an Advancements/Stats overlay opened with a key (Minecraft uses **L**), reusing the recipe-book's locked/unlocked grid layout and the existing pixel-icon rendering; an "Advancement unlocked" toast reusing the existing notification path.
- **Save** — one additive top-level field (likely bundling `stats` + `advancements`); should be a clean additive bump with a pure migration, no worldgen impact.

Two things I'll want your call on once I've confirmed the plumbing (I'll ask concretely then): **how broad the first advancement set should be** (a tight ~12–15 covering each major system, vs. a larger tree), and **where the panel lives** (a dedicated `L`-key overlay like Minecraft, vs. a new tab in the existing pause menu). I'll come back shortly with the grounded plan.

Both maps are in. The architecture fits this feature cleanly — here's the grounded plan.

## Design

**Engine (headless, testable):**
- Two new persistent fields on `GameState`: `stats: Map<string,number>` (counters) and `advancements: Set<string>` (unlocked ids) — both saved like `xp` and **death-proof** (restored at init, *not* cleared on death).
- **One chokepoint, not scattered edits.** Every gameplay event already funnels through `GameEngine.emit()` (29 event types — `blockBroken`, `mobDied`, `enchanted`, `drankPotion`, `bossDefeated`, `mobBred`, `fishingCaught`, …). I'll hook a single observer there: `recordEvent(state, event)` bumps the relevant counters, then `evaluateAdvancements(state)` unlocks any whose threshold is now met and emits a new `advancementUnlocked` event (guarded against re-entry). The 2–3 non-event stats (distance walked, play time) increment in the step loop. → no touching 15 system files.
- **Declarative registry** in a new `lib/game/engine/systems/advancements.ts`: each advancement is `{ id, title, description, icon, category, stat, threshold, parent? }`, so unlock logic is uniformly `stats.get(stat) >= threshold` — trivially unit-testable. Icons reuse existing item/block sprites via `itemIconUrl` (e.g. Diamonds!→diamond_ore, Enchanter→enchanting_table, Postmortal→dragon_heart) — **zero new assets**.
- Save **v12 → v13** (additive `stats` + `advancements`, pure `migrateSaveV12toV13`, no worldgen change). Snapshot projects both (ref-stable, like `effectsProjection`) for the panel.
- `advancementsOpen` flag + `toggleAdvancements` command + an **`L`** key in `inputController.ts` (mirrors the `I`/inventory flow, exits pointer lock).

**React shell:**
- New `AdvancementsPanel.tsx` overlay gated on `advancementsOpen`, with **Advancements** (locked/unlocked grid, reusing the recipe-book layout + `PixelImg`) and **Statistics** (counter list) tabs (PauseMenu tab pattern).
- The `advancementUnlocked` event drives an **"Advancement Unlocked: <name>"** toast via the existing `useMinecraftGame` event-drain loop (the `pickedUp` pattern), plus an optional audio chime in the director.

This lands as a `feat/advancements` branch, ~3–4 focused commits (engine+save+tests / UI+toast / audio / docs+CHANGELOG), following your usual branch→PR flow.

335	export function serializeEffects(effects: Map<EffectId, number>): SavedEffect[] {
336	  const out: SavedEffect[] = [];

338	    if (remaining > 0) out.push({ id, remaining });
339	  }

341	}

343	/** Reads active effects from a save, dropping unknown ids and non-positive/garbage durations. */
344	export function restoreEffects(save: SaveData): SavedEffect[] {
345	  if (!Array.isArray(save.effects)) return [];
346	  const out: SavedEffect[] = [];
347	  for (const entry of save.effects) {
348	    if (!entry || typeof entry.id !== "string" || !Object.hasOwn(VALID_EFFECT_IDS, entry.id)) continue;
349	    if (!Number.isFinite(entry.remaining) || entry.remaining <= 0) continue;
350	    out.push({ id: entry.id, remaining: entry.remaining });
351	  }
352	  return out;
353	}
354	
355	/**
356	 * Reads chest containers from a save into validated, CHEST_SLOTS-length slot
357	 * arrays keyed by voxel index. Per-slot validation only — the engine still
358	 * confirms each index actually holds a Chest block before using it.
359	 */
360	export function readContainers(save: SaveData): Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> {
361	  if (!Array.isArray(save.blockEntities)) return [];
362	  const out: Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> = [];
363	  for (const entry of save.blockEntities) {
364	    if (!entry || !Number.isFinite(entry.index) || !Array.isArray(entry.slots)) continue;
365	    const slots = Array.from({ length: CHEST_SLOTS }, (_, i) => restoreSlot(entry.slots[i]));
366	    out.push({ index: entry.index, slots });
367	  }
368	  return out;
369	}
370	
371	/**
372	 * Rebuilds inventory slots from a save, dropping unknown items, clamping
373	 * counts and durability, and skipping broken gear. Supports both the current
374	 * inventorySlots shape and the legacy inventoryCounts shape. Returns null when
375	 * the save carries no inventory.
376	 */
377	export function restoreInventorySlots(save: SaveData): InventorySlot[] | null {
378	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
379	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
380	    for (let i = 0; i < Math.min(INVENTORY_SLOTS, save.inventorySlots.length); i += 1) {
381	      slots[i] = restoreSlot(save.inventorySlots[i]);
382	    }
383	    return slots;
384	  }

The seams are confirmed, and I found the two things that shape the design:

1. **`GameApi` is minimal** (`dispatch` + `serialize` only) and **`refreshSnapshot` shallow-compares per key** — so I must *not* project the raw frequently-mutating `stats` Map into the snapshot (it'd either never re-render, or churn at 60Hz). The panel will read stats/advancements through a small new `GameApi` accessor at render time, and the snapshot carries only a cheap `advancementsOpen` flag + low-churn unlocked count.
2. **There's no `crafted` event** — so craft/brew/trade-based advancements need one small new event emitted in the `craft` dispatch handler.

> AGENT

The plan is written and grounded in the verified seams. Here's the summary before you approve.

## Advancements + Statistics — plan summary

**The gap:** the game is content-rich but goal-poor. This adds progression meta — ~15 advancements + a statistics screen — that surfaces and rewards every system already in the game.

**Engine (clean, one chokepoint):**
- New persistent `stats: Map` + `advancements: Set` on `GameState`, saved like `xp` and **death-proof**.
- A single observer hooked into `GameEngine.emit()` (the funnel all 29 events already pass through) — no editing 15 system files. `recordEvent` bumps counters; `evaluateAdvancements` unlocks any whose threshold is met and emits `advancementUnlocked`.
- A **declarative registry** (`{id, title, description, icon, stat, threshold}`) so unlocks are uniformly `stats.get(stat) >= threshold` — trivially testable. Icons reuse existing item sprites via `itemIconUrl` → **zero new assets**.
- One small new `crafted` event (no craft event exists today) to enable craft/brew/trade advancements.

**Persistence:** save **v12 → v13**, additive + pure migration, no worldgen change.

**UI:** an `L`-key overlay with Advancements (locked/unlocked grid) + Statistics tabs, reusing the recipe-book grid and PauseMenu tab patterns. Two design subtleties I confirmed against the code and accounted for: `refreshSnapshot` shallow-compares per key (so the panel reads stats through a new `GameApi.advancementState()` accessor at render time, not via a churning snapshot field), and the unlock toast comes from the event-drain loop (the `pickedUp` pattern), with a chime in the audio director.
